JESUS, KINGDOM AND POWER · II

Four things he kept doing

Authority
Crowds say he teaches "as one who had authority, and not as the scribes," and officials keep asking "By what authority are you doing these things?" He argues sabbath practice, calls scribes and Pharisees "blind guides" and "whitewashed tombs," and drives the money changers out of the temple court. For TKC: POSSIBLE: unlicensed authority is the crime Dominion can't forgive, since it's the only one that threatens the licensing system itself.
Bodies
He touches a man with a skin disease and takes a dead girl by the hand. The bleeding woman touches him first, without asking, and he commends her faith. INTERPRETATION The contagion runs the other way. He doesn't become unclean, and they become well. CONTESTED Whether that amounts to opposing the purity system. Paula Fredriksen and Amy-Jill Levine warn that reading Jesus as against Jewish law is a Christian habit with an ugly history. He sends the healed man to the priest with the offering Moses commanded.
Women
Mary of Bethany sits at his feet in the student's place, and he defends her. Named women fund the movement. A Syrophoenician woman argues with him over healing her daughter, answers his refusal with "even the dogs under the table eat the children's crumbs," and he grants it "for this saying." She wins the exchange. He's anointed by a woman in a room of men who call it waste. No gospel records him telling a woman to be silent.
Money
He tells a rich man to sell everything, and the man goes away grieving. Luke pairs blessings on the poor with woes on the rich and adds the rich fool and the rich man and Lazarus. In his first sermon, at Nazareth, he reads Isaiah 61, "release to the captives… the year of the Lord's favor," which echoes the Jubilee, and says it's fulfilled today. For TKC: INTERPRETATION: The most suppressed material in Dominion's scripture is economic more than sexual. Follow the money and you'll find the censorship.
CORRECTION TO THE OLD COMPANION The old companion said the Nazareth room "turns on him" over the year of release. The congregation first speaks well of him (4.22). They try to throw him off a cliff after he says Elijah and Elisha were sent to foreigners, a widow of Sidon and a Syrian general (4.24–30).

JESUS, KINGDOM AND POWER · THE LORD'S PRAYER · 1 OF 4

Thy kingdom come

The prayer appears twice, in different settings and forms. In Matthew it's part of the Sermon on the Mount, taught in place of heaping up empty phrases. In Luke the disciples ask "Lord, teach us to pray," and he gives a shorter version. Every pronoun for the people praying is plural. There's no "I" in it.

ClauseMatthew 6.9–13Luke 11.2–4Common liturgical form
AddressOur Father in heavenFatherOur Father, who art in heaven
NameHallowed be your nameHallowed be your nameHallowed be thy name
KingdomYour kingdom comeYour kingdom comeThy kingdom come
WillYour will be done, on earth as it is in heavenAbsent in the earliest manuscriptsThy will be done, on earth as it is in heaven
BreadGive us this day our daily breadGive us each day our daily breadGive us this day our daily bread
ForgivenessForgive us our debts, as we also have forgiven our debtorsForgive us our sins, for we ourselves forgive everyone indebted to usForgive us our trespasses, as we forgive those who trespass against us
TestingLead us not into temptation, but deliver us from evil (or "the evil one")Lead us not into temptationLead us not into temptation, but deliver us from evil
DoxologyAbsent in the earliest manuscriptsAbsentFor thine is the kingdom, and the power, and the glory, for ever. Amen.
CORRECTION TO THE OLD COMPANION The old companion presented one prayer and treated "on earth as in heaven" as part of the title line. "Thy kingdom come" is in both gospels. "On earth as in heaven" is Matthew's alone, the doxology is a later addition, and the prayer most people recite is Matthew's text with a liturgical ending.

415
JESUS, KINGDOM AND POWER · THE LORD'S PRAYER · 2 OF 4

Clause by clause

Our Father in heaven
A shared parent makes the people praying siblings, and rank inside the family a fiction. INTERPRETATION Authority is placed where no earthly office can occupy it. POSSIBLE Dominion prefers the clause said upward and never sideways.
Hallowed be your name
A request that God's name be treated as holy. INTERPRETATION It implies no other name is, imperial or dynastic. Ask what Dominion has carved above its doors.
Your kingdom come
The title line, in both gospels. It asks for the kingdom's arrival, which assumes it isn't fully here. The Jewish Kaddish, an Aramaic prayer whose core many date early, also pairs the hallowing of God's name with a prayer that the kingdom come soon, and the two are often compared.
Your will be done, on earth as in heaven
Matthew's. It's absent from Luke in the earliest manuscripts (P75, Codex Vaticanus) and was added by later copyists. INTERPRETATION In Matthew it ties the kingdom to the earth, to the place where taxes are collected and soldiers are stationed.
Our daily bread
Epiousios, "daily," appears nowhere in Greek before the gospels, and its meaning is uncertain: "for today," "for tomorrow," "necessary for existence." Jerome translated it two ways, "supersubstantial" in Matthew and "daily" in Luke. It asks for enough, and for "our" bread more than "my" bread.
KADDISH
EPIOUSIOS
JEROME, VULGATE
TYNDALE (1526)
BOOK OF COMMON PRAYER (1549)
MATT 6.14–15
416
JESUS, KINGDOM AND POWER · THE LORD'S PRAYER · 2 OF 4, CONTINUED

Clause by clause, continued

Forgive us our debts
Matthew says debts, opheilēmata. Luke says sins, though his second half keeps the debt language. Both tie the request to the one praying it. "Trespasses" isn't in either gospel's prayer. It comes from Tyndale's Matthew (1526) and the Book of Common Prayer (1549), and passed into Anglican and Catholic English. Matthew's very next verses do use a word for trespasses.
Lead us not into temptation
Peirasmos means testing or trial as well as temptation. INTERPRETATION A request to be spared the test, more than to pass it heroically. Pope Francis criticized the wording in 2017, and the French and Italian Catholic liturgies changed it. "From evil" can also be "from the evil one."
For thine is the kingdom
Absent from the earliest manuscripts of Matthew and from every early Luke. A shorter form appears in the Didache, about 100 CE. Later manuscripts added it, the King James printed it, and most modern translations footnote it.
CORRECTION TO THE OLD COMPANION The old companion said institutions render the clause "trespasses" to blunt its money sense. Debts, sins and trespasses track which text and translation tradition a church inherited, and they aren't evidence of motive. Churches that say "debts," Presbyterian and Reformed ones among them, follow the King James Matthew.

PART III · THE GOSPEL GRID
Luke 1.1–3
Many have undertaken to compile a narrative of the things that have been accomplished among us…
it seemed good to me also, having followed all things closely for some time past, to write an orderly account for you.
✥
A gospel that opens by saying there were others. Four, kept side by side.
REFERENCE UNIT · ◈
The Gospel Grid

There's no single gospel. There are four, written for different communities decades apart, and they disagree about the order of events, the wording of the sayings, the day of the death and what happened at the tomb. This unit holds all four at once.

Why a grid and not a harmony

A harmony stitches the four into one story and quietly deletes the differences. The differences are the most useful material in the New Testament for a writer. They show four groups deciding what mattered.

What this unit won't do

Tell you which account is right, whether the differences are contradictions or perspectives, or what any of it obliges you to believe. Every date and source theory is marked as argued.

SOURCE MARGIN
MATT · MARK · LUKE · JOHN
NA28 · UBS5
SANDERS, THE HISTORICAL FIGURE OF JESUS (1993)
EHRMAN, THE NEW TESTAMENT

The record on each

All four are formally anonymous. The names were attached by tradition, generally in the second century. Every date is a mainstream range, and every one is argued.

GOSPEL GRID · 3 OF 6 · WHO COPIED WHOM

The overlap problem

Matthew, Mark and Luke run parallel closely enough to print in columns, same episodes, same order, sometimes identical Greek for long stretches. Independent memory doesn't do that. Some literary relationship is certain. CONTESTED Which one.

ProposalWhat it claimsIts weak point
Two-source, the majority viewMatthew and Luke each used Mark, plus a lost sayings collection called Q, plus material of their ownQ has never been found and is reconstructed from the texts it explains
Farrer · Goodacre (2002)Luke used Mark and Matthew both, so no lost source is neededIt has to explain why Luke broke up Matthew's sermons and moved the pieces
Matthew first · GriesbachMatthew is earliest, and Mark condensed the othersIt has to explain why a condenser would add rough Greek and unflattering details
JohnKnew the other three, knew a tradition behind them, or wrote independently. A separate dispute.Its differences are too large to be editorial trimming
Only in Mark
A healing that takes two tries, a young man who runs away naked at the arrest, and the abrupt ending at the empty tomb.
Only in Matthew
The magi, the massacre of the children, the guard at the tomb, and the sheep and the goats.
Only in Luke
The shepherds, Mary's song, the good Samaritan, the prodigal son, the rich man and Lazarus, the road to Emmaus. Most of what's quoted about poverty and mercy is his.
Only in John
Cana, Nicodemus at night, the Samaritan woman, Lazarus raised, the foot washing and the long farewell.
The finding that matters for TKC

INTERPRETATION Whatever the order, later writers visibly edited earlier ones, softening, reordering, adding a birth and adding an ending. Scripture inside the canon is visibly revised scripture. An empire that claims a fixed, unedited text is claiming something its own documents contradict, and that's a device as much as a polemic.

GOSPEL GRID · 5 OF 6 · THE REAL DISAGREEMENTS

Where they don't match

These are differences in the text. Whether they're contradictions or complementary perspectives is a theological judgment this companion leaves to you. Their being differences isn't in dispute.

The temple action
In the first three it happens in the final week and triggers the arrest. In John it opens the ministry. Same event, opposite role.
The day of death
The first three make the last meal a Passover meal. John puts the crucifixion before the Passover meal, so the death coincides with the slaughter of the lambs.
The genealogies
Matthew and Luke give different lines with different names between David and Joseph, running in opposite directions. Reconciliations have been proposed, and none commands agreement.
After the birth
Matthew has a flight to Egypt to escape a massacre. Luke has a calm presentation at the temple and a return to Nazareth. The two itineraries are hard to run at once.
The beatitudes
Matthew sets them on a mountain and blesses the poor in spirit. Luke sets them on level ground, blesses the poor, and adds matching woes on the rich. One is interior and one is economic.
The prayer
Luke's is shorter, and the familiar closing line is missing from the oldest manuscripts of both. The prayer most recited in the world is a composite.
At the tomb
The number of women, the number of figures, whether the stone is already moved, who's told, Galilee or Jerusalem, and whether anyone speaks all differ. Women are the first witnesses in every one.
The one detail they all keep

INTERPRETATION Four accounts that agree on almost no logistics all put women at the tomb first, in a culture where women's testimony carried little legal weight. Some scholars argue an inventing community wouldn't have chosen it. It's an argument from plausibility and not a proof.

JOHN 19.14–31

The title, twice
Elias Vane's 2033 sermon is called Thy Kingdom Come (TL), and the novel takes the same words. INTERPRETATION Every character who prays the line is asking either for the Empire to last or for something to replace it, and may not know which.

POSSIBLE The Temple makes "My kingdom is not of this world" its favorite verse and then names the Empire as the kingdom come. The move needs both halves, the kingdom emptied of territory and the Empire claiming the territory.
On earth as in heaven
POSSIBLE The clause the Temple can't spiritualize, because it names the earth. Someone who's read Luke knows the prayer works without it, and someone who's read Matthew knows it doesn't.
Debts and levies
In Aurem, families of the top five Assessment finishers get the kind of review where unpaid levies disappear (DR3). INTERPRETATION The Empire forgives a household's debts in exchange for its daughter. POSSIBLE A character notices the Temple says "trespasses" in the same building as the levy office.

INTERPRETATION Twelve Covenant Daughters pray every week to be spared the test while the Empire tests them for four months. POSSIBLE The Temple teaches the clause as a prayer for the strength to pass.

No empire in this literature ever had to change a word of scripture. It only had to decide which words were read aloud, to whom, and how often.

EMPIRE AND INTERPRETATION · I · EMPIRES OF THE BIBLE

Five occupations

Almost the whole Bible was written by people living under someone else's government. Read the empires as a menu of methods, because Dominion uses some combination of them.

Egypt · labor
Forced labor, rising quotas and a decree controlling births. Unrest is answered with more work. Its method is exhaustion, people too tired to organize. Read Exodus 1–14.
Assyria · submission, advertised
Terror and deportation. Conquered peoples were broken up and resettled among strangers, and the northern kingdom disappeared this way. Its envoy shouts terms at Jerusalem's wall in the local language so the people on the wall can hear. Read 2 Kings 17–19 and Nahum.
Babylon · the top of a society
It deported the educated and skilled, trained young men in its own language and literature, renamed them and gave them careers, and left the poor to work the land. It also besieged Jerusalem, burned the temple and blinded the king, so it used both methods. Read 2 Kings 24–25, Daniel 1–6 and Lamentations.
Persia · order at low cost
Licensed and funded local religion. It sent the exiles home, paid for the temple and authorized its law. Its decrees can't be revoked. Its court gathers young women by search and removes a queen by legislation. Read Ezra, Nehemiah, Esther and Daniel 6.
Rome · taxes and quiet
Governed through local clients, a client king, a temple aristocracy and a governor who signs orders he disagrees with. Crucifixion was public and slow, by roads. The emperor was honored as divine and his rule called peace. Read the gospels, Acts and Revelation.
CORRECTION TO THE OLD COMPANION The old companion called Babylon's elite capture "far cheaper than terror," as if it chose one. It used both (2 Kgs 25.7–9).
The composite for Dominion

POSSIBLE The Empire uses the cheap methods, Babylon's elite capture, Persia's licensed religion and Rome's local clients. The Imperial Education Track takes exceptional common boys and makes them Crown-adjacent for life (MEN), which is Daniel 1. Terror is kept for the edges: the Ungoverned, Azrael, the 2061 removal (TL, FR 35–36).

EMPIRE AND INTERPRETATION · II · WHO GETS TO SAY WHAT IT MEANS

Seven separate powers

Authority over a text is a set of separate powers, usually held by different people. A religious state becomes total when one institution holds all of them. Work out which ones Dominion holds, because the ones it doesn't are where the plot lives.

PowerWhat it decidesHistorical case
The canonWhich books countSettled for most churches long ago, and so invisible
The translationWhich wordsWilliam Tyndale was executed in 1536, in part for translating the Bible into English without license
The lectionaryWhich passages are read aloud, on which daysMost worshippers hear only what the calendar of readings allows
The commentaryWhat a passage is taken to meanThe Geneva Bible's margin notes (1560) were one reason James I wanted a new translation without them
The pulpitWho may stand up and speakThe licensing of preachers, and its withdrawal
The copyWho owns a book, and which editionAbridged editions like the 1807 Slave Bible
LiteracyWho can read it unaidedSouth Carolina's Negro Act of 1740 banned teaching enslaved people to write, and several Southern states tightened anti-literacy laws after Nat Turner's revolt in 1831
The historical pattern

INTERPRETATION Movements that broke a religious monopoly usually did it by attacking translation, literacy or the copy. Reform tends to arrive as a printing problem before it's a theological one.

What the Empire holds

The pulpit (the Temple and its ministers), the lectionary (what's read before the scores, DR3), the commentary (the Assessment's doctrine section, DR3) and much of literacy (the Designation, DOM). OPEN QUESTION Does it hold the translation and the copy? Is the Book of Covenant a full Bible, and who may own another one?

EMPIRE AND INTERPRETATION · V · AMERICAN HISTORY · 1 OF 7

The city on a hill

The Puritans who settled Massachusetts Bay in 1630 understood their colony as a community bound to God by covenant, on the model of Israel in Deuteronomy. Blessing would follow faithfulness, and judgment would follow breach. "A Model of Christian Charity" is traditionally attributed to John Winthrop and dated to 1630, given aboard or beside the Arbella. CONTESTED Some scholars suggest another minister wrote it, and it wasn't printed until 1838.

It says the colony "shall be as a city upon a hill," borrowing Matthew 5.14, and its point is that the whole world will watch, so failure will be public. It closes by paraphrasing Deuteronomy 30: "Therefore let us choose life." Daniel Rodgers (As a City on a Hill, 2018) argues the sermon was little known until the twentieth century. John F. Kennedy quoted it to the Massachusetts legislature as president-elect on January 9, 1961. Ronald Reagan made "a shining city upon a hill" a theme of his speeches and the center of his farewell address on January 11, 1989, adding the word "shining." INTERPRETATION: In Winthrop's use the image is a warning about being watched. In later political use it became a description of national greatness.

163019611989
For TKC
The Covenant Coalition's platform was "America was great when it was godly. Return to God. Return to greatness" (TL). INTERPRETATION The Coalition reads Winthrop the way later politicians did and drops his point, that a covenant people is watched and judged by its failures. POSSIBLE Elias's 2033 sermon quotes "choose life" and Deuteronomy 30 together.
MARGINALIA
✥ See also: Deep dive IV, Deuteronomy, page 13.

EMPIRE AND INTERPRETATION · V · AMERICAN HISTORY · 2 OF 7

One Bible, two readings of slavery

Both sides of the American slavery debate read the same Bible and claimed it.

The proslavery case
Noah's curse on Canaan, read as a curse on Africans. The permanent slavery of foreigners in Leviticus 25.44–46. Abraham's household slaves. The instructions to slaves in Ephesians 6.5, Colossians 3.22 and 1 Peter 2.18. Paul sending Onesimus back in Philemon. And the claim that Jesus never condemned slavery.
Richard Furman
After the Denmark Vesey plot in Charleston in 1822, the Baptist minister wrote an exposition, dated that December, for the South Carolina Baptist Convention defending slavery from scripture. Southern defenders used its arguments until the Civil War.
Thornton Stringfellow
A Virginia Baptist pastor who published A Brief Examination of Scripture Testimony on the Institution of Slavery (Richmond, 1841).
The abolitionist answer
The Golden Rule, "God made of one blood all nations" (Acts 17.26, KJV), "there is neither bond nor free" (Gal 3.28), the capital law against kidnapping a person to sell (Exod 21.16), the refusal to return runaways (Deut 23.15–16), and the exodus itself.
Angelina Grimké
From a South Carolina slaveholding family, she published An Appeal to the Christian Women of the South (1836) through the American Anti-Slavery Society and made the Bible her test.
Frederick Douglass
Added an appendix to his Narrative (1845) to make clear his attacks were on "the slaveholding religion of this land," which he set against "the Christianity of Christ."
The crisis underneath

Mark Noll argues the war exposed a crisis in how Protestants read the Bible. Both sides held scripture to be authoritative and plain, and neither could persuade the other from it.

EMPIRE AND INTERPRETATION · V · AMERICAN HISTORY · 3 OF 7

The churches split, and the Slave Bible

The split
Methodists divided in 1844. The Southern Baptist Convention was founded in Augusta, Georgia, in 1845, after the national Baptist mission board refused to appoint slaveholders as missionaries. Presbyterians divided in stages, finally in 1861. In June 1995, meeting in Atlanta, the Southern Baptist Convention adopted a resolution repenting of its past racism and apologizing to African Americans.
The 1807 "Slave Bible"
A London missionary society published Select Parts of the Holy Bible, for the Use of the Negro Slaves, in the British West-India Islands. According to Anthony Schmidt of the Museum of the Bible, it kept 232 of the 1,189 chapters in a standard Protestant Bible, leaving out about ninety percent of the Old Testament and about half the New. The exodus is gone. Passages that reinforce slavery, Ephesians 6.5 among them, are kept. A copy on loan from Fisk University was shown at the museum in 2018–2019. The name "Slave Bible" is modern.

INTERPRETATION The clearest case on record of controlling scripture without changing a word, by deciding which words are in the copy.

EMPIRE AND INTERPRETATION · V · AMERICAN HISTORY · 4 OF 7

The Black church

The invisible institution
Under slavery much Black Christian worship was what the sociologist E. Franklin Frazier called the "invisible institution," held in secret, often in woods and gullies known as hush harbors, beyond white supervision and the laws against Black gatherings. Albert Raboteau's Slave Religion (1978) is the standard history.
Philadelphia
Richard Allen and other Black worshippers walked out of St. George's Methodist Church over segregated seating. Allen dated it to 1787, and some historians argue for 1792. He founded Mother Bethel in 1794, and in 1816 the African Methodist Episcopal Church became an independent denomination with Allen as its first bishop.
Charleston
The African church that became Emanuel AME was burned by white authorities in 1822 after Denmark Vesey, one of its founders, was accused of planning a revolt. Its members worshiped in secret until 1865. On June 17, 2015, a white supremacist killed nine people at a Bible study in the same church.
What grew from it
C. Eric Lincoln and Lawrence Mamiya describe seven historically Black denominations (1990). The spirituals, the exodus as the central story, the preaching tradition and the civil rights movement's base in churches, from the Southern Christian Leadership Conference in 1957 to King's last sermon, all come out of this history.
For TKC

INTERPRETATION Worship held out of sight of the authorities, in places the cameras don't reach. The Grace Bridge (CR) and the outer districts' kitchens (DOM) are the Empire's hush harbors. Douglass set "the slaveholding religion of this land" against "the Christianity of Christ," and Celeste's faith is on his side of that line. POSSIBLE Aurelia's outer districts remember the hush harbors, the burned church at Charleston and the walkout at St. George's, and the Temple teaches none of it.

EMPIRE AND INTERPRETATION · V · AMERICAN HISTORY · 5 OF 7

Christian nationalism

CONTESTED, as a term and in its boundaries.

A definition
The sociologists Andrew Whitehead and Samuel Perry define it as "a cultural framework… that idealizes and advocates a fusion of Christianity with American civic life," and argue the "Christian" in it is more an identity than a faith.
A measurement
A 2023 PRRI and Brookings survey built a scale from five statements about Christianity and American law and society. It classed about 10 percent of American adults as adherents and 19 percent as sympathizers, with 39 percent skeptics and 29 percent rejecters. Among white evangelical Protestants, 29 percent were adherents and 35 percent sympathizers.
The objection
Critics, the political scientist Mark David Hall among them, argue the definitions are too broad and sweep in ordinary religious participation in politics.
The embrace
Some on the right have taken the term for themselves. Stephen Wolfe's The Case for Christian Nationalism (2022) argues for it openly.
For TKC

INTERPRETATION The Coalition, as the timeline describes it, megachurches, political action groups and militias running candidates at every level (TL), fits Whitehead and Perry's definition closely. It's a reading of your own invented history, and the real-world use of the term is CONTESTED.

Who in the founding generation read Winthrop, Douglass and Rushdoony, and what they took from each

EMPIRE AND INTERPRETATION · V · AMERICAN HISTORY · 6 OF 7

Reconstruction, dominion, seven mountains

Christian Reconstructionism
R. J. Rushdoony founded the Chalcedon Foundation in 1965 and published The Institutes of Biblical Law in 1973, arguing that God's law in scripture, the Torah's case laws included, should be the standard for civil law. Greg Bahnsen's Theonomy in Christian Ethics (1977) gave the position its name, and Gary North extended it into economics. Its most distinctive claim is that the Mosaic civil penalties remain valid, and its writers listed the death penalty for offenses including adultery, homosexual acts, blasphemy and witchcraft. CONTESTED How consistently Rushdoony applied the penalties. Most evangelical and Reformed theologians rejected theonomy, and Westminster Seminary's faculty published a critique in 1990. Rushdoony's influence on Christian schooling and homeschooling was large and is acknowledged on both sides.
Dominionism
CONTESTED, as a label. Sara Diamond described "dominion theology" in 1989, and Frederick Clarkson and others used "dominionism" for Christian Right groups that read Genesis 1.28 as a mandate for Christian control of public life. Critics call it a smear used too loosely.
RUSHDOONY, INSTITUTES OF BIBLICAL LAW (1973)
BAHNSEN, THEONOMY (1977)
THEONOMY: A REFORMED CRITIQUE (1990)
DIAMOND, SPIRITUAL WARFARE (1989)
TAYLOR, THE VIOLENT TAKE IT BY FORCE (2024)

EMPIRE AND INTERPRETATION · V · AMERICAN HISTORY · 6 OF 7, CONTINUED

Reconstruction, dominion, seven mountains, continued

The Seven Mountains
The mandate holds that Christians are called to influence or lead seven spheres: family, religion, education, media, arts and entertainment, government and business. Proponents trace it to a 1975 meeting where Loren Cunningham and Bill Bright each received the same list, framed as evangelism. After Lance Wallnau heard it from Cunningham in 2000 and was mentored by C. Peter Wagner of the New Apostolic Reformation, it took a more political turn. Some trace it back to Abraham Kuyper's "sphere sovereignty." Matthew D. Taylor traces the movement's networks and their role on January 6, 2021, including the Appeal to Heaven flag.
For TKC

POSSIBLE Whoever named the Dominions held the dominionist reading of Genesis 1.28 and chose the word because of it. The Triune Rule puts three of the seven mountains, religion, government and media, into the constitution as the Temple, the Crown and the Broadcast (TL), and the founders may have thought of the Trials as taking the "family" mountain. The Covenant Court may cite theonomist arguments for Imperial penalties and quietly decline the parts of Mosaic law that would bind the Crown.

Founded in 1987. It published its egalitarian statement, "Men, Women and Biblical Equality," in 1989, and it reached a wide audience in Christianity Today in 1990.

THE FALL OF THE UNITED STATES · HOW TO USE THIS PART

The world starts here

The United States became the Covenant Empire of America over about eighteen years. It started with a stadium sermon in 2033 and ended in 2051, when the Crown, the Temple and the Broadcast were written into the constitution as the three branches of government. Almost every step went through a vote, a referendum or an agreement (TL). Nobody in Eden's lifetime remembers the country before. The oldest person in her cast was born around 2035, into its last few years.

From this part on, the companion is about your world, and the rules change slightly. Anything your files establish carries a source tag and no label. Everything else is labeled at the start of the sentence it governs: INFERENCE for what the files imply without saying, POSSIBLE for ideas that aren't canon, OPEN QUESTION for what you still have to decide, CONFLICT where two files disagree, SUPERSEDED where older material no longer controls, and DECIDED for a call you've made. The Final Rulings outrank everything. Where a file and a ruling disagree, the ruling wins and the page says so.

Source tags
TL Timeline · TR Trials · TR-orig original Trials file · DOM Dominions · MEN Men in the Empire · CR Crownward · DR3 Draft Three · WR Writing Rules · FR Final Rulings · AM Audit Master · Ruling, the October 23 calibration
Decided canon
Solomon is sixteen. Leah is younger than Eden. Thomas left voluntarily (FR 3). Celeste has no proof Miriam is alive (FR 16). Leah sat for an assessment.
MARGINALIA
✥ See also: The canon register, at the back.

THE FALL · I · THE TIMELINE · 1 OF 2

2025 to 2051

YearEventSource
2025–2031The Fracture Years. A contested election leaves two men claiming the presidency at once. Automated labor wipes out forty percent of working-class jobs. Domestic attacks on federal buildings go uncontained, and a climate disaster makes the Gulf Coast uninhabitable. Faith in federal institutions collapses, and church attendance triples.TL
2031Evangelical megachurches, conservative political action groups and private militias organize as the Covenant Coalition. "America was great when it was godly. Return to God. Return to greatness." They run candidates at every level at once.TL
2033Elias Vane, a little-known Georgia preacher, preaches Thy Kingdom Come for six hours to 400,000 people at The Gathering House, the former Mercedes-Benz Stadium in Atlanta. Every Coalition network carries it live.TL
2035Vane wins a Senate seat from Georgia, and within eighteen months he effectively controls the legislative agenda. Celeste Parish is born about now (INFERENCE).TL
2039California, Oregon and New York attempt to secede. The federal government calls it treason, and a two-year standoff follows.TL
2041The Partition Agreement brings the three states back with "regional autonomy," dismantled about four years later. Vane's Restoration Act passes Congress narrowly, under conditions later described as coercion.TL
2043Vane declares the United States dissolved and reborn as the Covenant Empire of America and takes the title First Elector. The Crownward is established over Washington, D.C.TL, CR
2044The Dominion Act makes the states Twelve Dominions, each under a Governor-Prelate. Atlanta becomes Aurelia, seat of Aurem.TL
2048Elias, 52, marries Sera Callum, 28, a Vowed woman and Temple scholar. The first Anointed marriage.TL
2051The Triune Rule makes the Crown, the Temple and the Broadcast the three constitutional governing bodies.TL

THE FALL · II · HOW THE EMPIRE TELLS IT

Official version, lived reality

Everyone in Eden's world learned the left column in school. Where a character sits between the two columns says a lot about who she is. The Empire's phrasing is what people would actually say aloud, so it's the version to use in dialogue (AM).

THE FALL · III · THE RESTORATION ACT OF 2041

The legal root

The Restoration Act is the legal root of almost everything in Eden's life. It passed Congress, so the Empire's founding argument is that nothing illegal happened (TL).

AUTHORED BY SENATOR ELIAS VANE OF GEORGIA · PASSED BY THE CONGRESS OF THE UNITED STATES

conditional on standing, compliance and designation
Title II · Marriage
a legal and theological designation

"reorganized for accuracy and unity"
Preamble
________________________________
Ten years after Title IV the same apparatus is a branch of government, and today it controls forty percent of a Covenant Daughter's score. The Binding, the Commonwife quota and the Anointed marriage all descend from Title II. Unbound is a legal category with a file because of Title III.

THE FALL · IV · LIVING MEMORY

Grandmothers and documents

Nobody in the cast lived through the Fall as an adult. What survives comes down through grandmothers and through the House of Vane's own papers.

Celeste Parish · TL
About nine in 2044, she remembers the renaming of Atlanta and her own grandmother crying when they changed the signs. She watched the Empire take her mother's identity through a Commonwife marriage, then raised Miriam alone under Unbound designation rather than let it happen again. The real name of the city is the last thing she says to Eden before the Crownward. (Volume Two, Celeste.)
Sera Montoya's grandmother · TR
She was at the last protest before the Partition Agreement ended Pacific's resistance in 2041. She tells it to Sera as a warning and not a hero story: the difference between impact and loss is knowing when and how.
Pacific's generations · DOM
The people who remember trying to leave raised children who grew up with parents who'd tried and failed, and their grandchildren grew up knowing the story. The Empire still checks every Pacific girl's Designation for signs that the secession story survived in her.
Zara Voss's grandmother · TR
She has a real name the desert knows and the Empire doesn't. She raised Zara in Sable Reach and the desert beyond it.
The House of Vane · TL, FR 35–44
The family keeps documents instead of grandmothers' stories, and those documents are the most dangerous things in the Crownward.
Celeste, to Eden, before the Crownward
The real name of the city.

Elias's son. He took the throne at 34, so he was born around 2028, twenty years before his father's Anointed marriage (INFERENCE). Cold and procedural. He manages people instead of inspiring them, and under him the Empire grows more efficient and more brutal (TL).

THE FALL · V · FOUNDER FILES · THE REMOVED HEIR AND DORIAN

The removed heir and Dorian Vane

The removed heir
Elias's elder child, and the rightful successor. Removed in 2061, when Dorian was still a child (FR 35–36). OPEN QUESTION What did the heir know or do that made removal necessary? The rulings cover how it happened and why Dorian was spared, and not why the heir had to go.
Dorian Vane · Emperor since 2085
The removed heir's son. He took the throne at 40 and is 59 in 2104 (TL, Ruling). He was spared because a child's claim can be supervised. Removing him would have needed a public reason, and raising him inside the house needed none (FR 38). He has known since 2061 that the documents name Elias (FR 44). Under him the Broadcast became genuinely sophisticated, and the Trials became the most-watched event of the year (TL).

THE FALL · V · FOUNDER FILES · THE LEDGER AND THE DISSENT

The ledger and the Covenant of Dissent

2098
During a Crown advisory access period, Azrael found a ledger in the east wing archive. It ties the Empire's founding documents to a series of deliberate removals, which the Covenant of Purpose describes as God's order and the ledger describes as something else. It had sat there unmoved since 2085, because nobody was looking (FR 41).
Put it back
He took it to his father, who told him to put it back. Azrael kept it and went looking for its companion, the Covenant of Dissent, which Elias wrote in the same year and the same hand, as the theological counter-argument to everything the Empire was built on.
2100
Dorian removed both documents the week Azrael was declared dead (FR 42). He'd told Azrael to put the ledger back because a document Azrael had read and returned would be one Dorian controlled. Destroying it would have told Azrael it mattered (FR 43).

BUILDING THE EMPIRE · I · THE TRIUNE RULE

Three bodies, one score

In 2051 the Crown, the Temple and the Broadcast were made the three governing bodies of the Empire. What had worked informally since 2043 became constitutional, and the Broadcast stopped being a network of Coalition media companies and became a branch of government (TL).

The CrownHouse of Vane · Phase Two · 30%
The TempleSanctum · Vowed · Phase One · 30%
The BroadcastCitadel · Saint Meridian · 40%
Governor-Prelatesone per Dominion
The Registryevery citizen's paper
The Vowedassessment · observation
Covenant Courtthe law, interpreted
How the Trials are split
The Temple runs Phase One and the Crown runs Phase Two. Endurance belongs to the terrain. The Covenant Score is weighted 30 percent Temple, 30 percent Crown and 40 percent Broadcast (TR).
The secret
The Broadcast's share is never disclosed, because the public would be angry to learn that a broadcaster outweighs the Church in choosing the Empire's most powerful woman (TR).
Who in the cast knows the forty percent, and how they found out
TKC: TL · TR · CR

BUILDING THE EMPIRE · II · THE CROWN AND THE HOUSE OF VANE

Four rulers, three methods

Every Emperor descends from Elias Vane, who founded the House in 2054 (TL). The Temple teaches that God chose Elias and that the bloodline carries the anointing forward, so the family's status is theological as well as political (CR).

RulerYearsHow he held powerSource
Elias VaneFirst Elector 2043, Emperor 2054–2061Belief. A preacher who made people want what he wanted.TL, AM
Crest Vane2062–2085Procedure: quotas, designations, criminal categories and the TrialsTL
Dorian Vane2085 to now, 59 in 2104Performance. He brought back the spectacle and made the Trials the year's biggest event.TL, Ruling
Judah Elijah VaneCrown Heir, 25 in 2104He hasn't yet. He was raised by the Temple as much as by his parents.TL
Succession
By survivorship and the absence of any other recognized claimant (FR 39). Crest inherited after his elder sibling was removed in 2061. Dorian, that sibling's son, inherited after Crest died (FR 35, 39). When Azrael was declared dead in 2100, the line passed to Judah (TL).
The heir's marriage
A matter of state. Dorian has pushed Judah toward an Anointed Wife since he turned 23, because an unmarried heir is an heir whose loyalties are still unwritten (CR). Elite men usually marry between 25 and 35. The Trials exist partly because of Judah's reluctance, to make the choice feel like an honor instead of pressure (MEN).
Advisory access
Young members of the House take part in "Crown advisory access periods," which is how Azrael got legitimate access to the east wing archive (TL, FR 41).
Lyris Vane
Empress, 58, and the most sophisticated political operator in the Empire. She's spent thirty years performing perfect Anointed Wifehood while running the Crownward's domestic and diplomatic machinery, and lobbied through Dorian for a decade to shape the Anointed Wife's legal protections, framing every gain as his gift (CR). She came from Mountain Dominion (WR).

SUPERSEDED The Crownward file calls Crest Dorian's father. Under FR 35, Crest is Dorian's uncle.

TKC: TL · AM · RULING · FR 35 · FR 39 · FR 41 · CR · MEN · WR

BUILDING THE EMPIRE · III · THE CROWNWARD · 1 OF 2

The capital

The Crownward is built over Washington, D.C., formally established in 2043. Elias occupied the old capital instead of moving it, because you don't abandon the seat of the thing you claim to restore. Most people in 2104 have never seen a photograph of Washington (CR). The Potomac is now the Covenant River, and it divides the city.

The bridges
The Sovereign Bridge is the widest and appears on the currency. The Covenant Bridge is narrower and used by staff. The Grace Bridge is the oldest, never fully renovated, and has blind spots in Broadcast coverage, so it's where people go when they don't want to be filmed.
RESEARCH
In the real city central Washington sits on the Potomac's east bank, and the west bank is Arlington, Virginia. Read literally, the Sovereign Side stands where Arlington National Cemetery and the Pentagon are now. POSSIBLE Somebody in the Crownward knows the Crown Palace is built over the old national cemetery.

BUILDING THE EMPIRE · III · THE CROWNWARD · 2 OF 2

The order nobody writes down

The Crownward has a rigid social order that nobody writes down and everybody enforces (CR).

The House of Vane
At the top.
The Anointed Court
About forty families whose standing is measured by how close they live to the Palace. They attend quarterly audiences with the Emperor, and the Temple High Prelates sit among them.
The Administrative Class
The senior staff of the Crown, Temple and Broadcast. Most of their wives hold Stewardwife designation.
Their daughters
The Crownward's daughters who enter the Trials are rarely the most celebrated candidates, and they're almost always the most dangerous (CR).
What someone on the Grace Bridge can do that they can't do anywhere else
A walk across the Crownward, west to east, in Eden's first week

BUILDING THE EMPIRE · IV · THE TEMPLE AND THE VOWED

Ministers, Vowed and the Prelate

The Temple is a church and a branch of government. In Eden's world it shows up as ministers, Vowed women and doctrine. Its scripture is read from the Book of Covenant (DR3), and the Temple Sanctum in the Crownward is its seat (CR).

Ministers
Senior Minister Bell is the model: a man who signs Binding certificates, sponsors Assessment candidates, and prays God will close every door not intended for a girl (DR3).
The Vowed
Women in Temple service, who run most of the Empire's contact with women. A Vowed officer assesses every Aurem girl at eighteen (DOM). They conduct the quarterly household assessments in Aurem's Commonwife homes (MEN), run the Regional Assessments, and observe, examine and interview the Covenant Daughters (TR). In Red Earth they attend school performances as "pastoral care" (DOM).
The Crownward Vowed
The Temple's elite and the Empire's most efficient intelligence network. The Temple calls it spiritual discernment and the Crown uses it as surveillance, and it's never written down anywhere the Empire could confiscate it (CR).
Leaving
The designation can be left through marriage. Delphine's mother went from Vowed to Bonded Wife, and Hannah was Vowed before she became House Mother (TR, FR 30). Sera Callum was Vowed when Elias married her (TL). Hannah's daughter Naomi has one year left in Vowed education (TL).
TKC: DR3 · CR · DOM · MEN · TR · TL · FR 30 · FR 33

BUILDING THE EMPIRE · IV · THE TEMPLE AND THE VOWED, CONTINUED

The Prelate

PORTRAIT · THE PRELATE · WORKING NAME AURELIA
The Prelate

She heads the Crownward's Vowed. She's 67, has served three Emperors, and knows better than anyone in the city how power actually works. She assessed Judah's spiritual formation personally. In the Trial's first week she'll reach a conclusion about Eden that she shares with no one, the Emperor included. She anoints the Crown Heir's bride (CR).

The files call her Prelate Naomi. FR 33 renames her so Hannah's daughter keeps the name. POSSIBLE Prelate Aurelia is the working name. She is never Huldah.

What the Prelate concludes about Eden in the first week, and what she does with it

BUILDING THE EMPIRE · V · THE BROADCAST, THE GOVERNORS, THE REGISTRY

Paper and cameras

The Broadcast
State media and the third branch. Its Citadel stands in the Crownward, and its studios, where Trial scores are announced, sit in old Midtown Manhattan in Saint Meridian (DOM). It holds 40 percent of the Covenant Score, including "narrative priority decisions" by the Broadcast Director. It edits the Civic Debates for Dominion release and builds a story around every candidate in real time. When a candidate's intelligence becomes the story instead of her compliance, it runs the "Eve narrative," which implies and never accuses (TR).
Governor-Prelates
One political and religious office per Dominion (TL). They choose each Covenant Daughter from the top three of the Regional Assessment, and in practice take whoever the Crown has quietly signaled it prefers (TR). Gulf's first lasted fourteen months. Lone's has always been born in Texas. Pacific's was appointed by Dorian. Ardenne's has mostly stopped trying to fix the marriage records from outside Ashford (DOM).
The Registry
On the Administrative Side. Every citizen's Imperial record: birth designations, marriage registrations, Covenant Scores, Designation results and deaths. Only the Crown Palace is guarded more heavily. Whoever controls what the Registry says about you controls something more basic than law, faith or the Broadcast, and Eden will understand this before most people in the story (CR).
The Covenant Court
Interprets Imperial law, with offices on the Administrative Side and secondary chambers in Blessed Shore, where the law "gets interpreted, refined, and weaponized" (CR, DOM).
Surveillance
Cameras and screens everywhere in the seat cities. The Covenant Houses are wired in every common area but not the bedrooms, a deliberate illusion of privacy that keeps candidates manageable (CR). Pacific has more cameras per block than any other Dominion. Eden's file reads "Exceptional intellect. Monitor closely," later "Selected for Trial. Monitor closely" (TL).
The gaps
The Grace Bridge, the Ardenne hollows, the Sable desert, west Lone's distances, Saint Meridian's density, and the Mountain terrain where Endurance runs mostly without cameras (CR, DOM, TR).
TKC: CR · DOM · TR · TL · DR3 · MEN

The authority line
Household forms demand an adult male authority. Celeste wrote "deceased" on the line, and the form came back with the word circled and a request for the next adult male (DR3). Solomon's vocational form kept coming back to the house until Zion, at eighteen, declared himself the authority (TL, DR3).
Reclassifying
A woman reclassifying her household under her own registration has to prove the arrangement is permanent (TL). Celeste's took six years.
Moving
Moving between Dominions requires Crown authorization, technically available and practically difficult (MEN).
Citations
Any Ungoverned citation disqualifies a woman from the Covenant Registry (TR). OPEN QUESTION What else does it do? Criminal record, designation, or both?
Deaths
Registered within 48 hours in the Crownward and 24 in Meridian (CR, DOM).

BUILDING THE EMPIRE · VI · MEN, LABOR AND THE ECONOMY

Control by structure

The Empire controls women through designation and men through structure. Men are told they're free, and the control sits in housing, careers and marriage approvals (MEN).

ClassWho they areMarriage
Common menThe labor and service class, registered to their DominionExpected by 21. Unmarried men past 22 stay in labor dormitories. Within caste and region.
Bonded menMid-tier administrators and skilled professionals, steered by Crown labor allocationBetween 21 and 25, from Unbound and Commonwife-eligible women, with Crown approval
Covenant menCrown officers, Temple leadership, Broadcast directors, senior militaryBetween 25 and 35. Their wives are Anointed.
Conscript menCommon boys whose scores flag them. Officially they don't exist.Crown approval at the Covenant Man level, for life
The Imperial Education Track
A common boy whose Designation scores come back exceptional gets a visit from a Crown education officer within three months, offering a scholarship. The family can decline, and declining routes him to the worst labor for life. Accepting makes him Crown-adjacent for life (MEN). (Volume Two, Solomon.)
The Covenant Declaration
At 18, common men register a public statement of loyalty and readiness for service and marriage. Elite men register theirs at the end of the Track (MEN).
Twelve economies
Irongate manufacturing. Lone oil and space infrastructure. Mountain classified operations, extraction and military capacity. Gulf tourism, rebranded "cultural pilgrimage." Meridian law and administration. Vane the Broadcast (DOM).

INFERENCE The forty percent of working-class jobs lost to automation in the Fracture Years never came back, which is what makes Imperial service look like opportunity to a common man.

BUILDING THE EMPIRE · VII · WHAT THE TEMPLE TEACHES

The official religion, in one line

Preached every week in every Temple
The husband leads, the wife supports, the household reflects divine order in miniature.
✥
THE OFFICIAL THEOLOGY OF MARRIAGE (CR)
The Vane anointing
God chose Elias to restore the nation, and the bloodline carries divine sanction (CR). Elias was canonized within a year, "not quite sainthood but close enough" (TL).
The House judges fairly
Bell tells Celeste "The House always judges fairly," and Celeste answers "The House calls whom it calls." Before the scores Bell speaks about "divine order, talent as stewardship, and elevation as burden," and closes with "May every daughter receive with grace the portion appointed to her" (DR3).
The House virtues
The banners in the Aurelia Temple atrium read obedience, discernment, modesty, stewardship and endurance (DR3).
Selection as revelation
The Broadcast frames the Trials as a revelation and never a competition: anointing, covenant, calling, worthy, chosen. A competition can be rigged, and a revelation can't (TR).
Being Bound as service
"Every girl in Aurem wanted to be Bound. It was the only way they knew how to serve." Covenant Candidacy is "the closest thing a woman could get to godliness" (DR3).

BUILDING THE EMPIRE · VII · THE FOUNDING TEXTS

Four documents

Thy Kingdom Come · 2033
Six hours, preached by Elias at The Gathering House. Required curriculum in every Dominion school since 2061 (TL). INTERPRETATION The old companion reads it as a familiar prayer taken literally in public for the first time. Nobody in the stadium heard a coup. They heard their own prayer said aloud by a man who wasn't embarrassed by it (AM).
The Covenant of Purpose
A founding document. It describes a series of deliberate removals as God's order, and the east wing ledger describes the same removals as something else (TL). Celeste can recite it (AM).
The Covenant of Dissent
Written by Elias in the same hand and the same year, as the theological counter-argument to everything the Empire was built on. Catalogued until 2100, when it disappeared the same week as the ledger. Judah has been looking for it since before his brother's exile (TL).
The Book of Covenant
The book Bell reads from at the Assessment. It includes Proverbs 31.29, "Many daughters have done virtuously, but thou excellest them all," in King James wording (DR3). OPEN QUESTION A full Protestant Bible, an abridged edition on the 1807 model, or scripture bound with the Empire's own founding texts?

BUILDING THE EMPIRE · VII · LIVED RELIGION · 6 OF 6

Faith that isn't performance

INFERENCE Where the files don't say how the Empire treats a practice, the column reads "Unregulated."

The blind spot

The files don't make every believer a dupe. Heartland faith is genuine, and its women call their questions about the Commonwife designation theological inquiry and mean it (DOM). Ruth Gideon "believes the Empire more than Eden does," sincere without being foolish (WR 26). Eden's own faith is real and "required more maintenance" than Maribel Vale's (DR3). Celeste is the most religious person in the book and the least Imperial (AM). INTERPRETATION The Empire approves what it's already lost and tolerates what makes money. What women do in kitchens, at bedsides and the night before a wedding mostly goes unseen. Celeste's whole faith is built in that blind spot, and the novel's religious line runs between faith the Temple can use and faith that answers to something it doesn't control.

TKC: DOM · WR 26 · DR3 · AM

Since the Restoration Act of 2041, a woman's citizenship has depended on her marital status (TL). The Empire controls women by telling each one what she is and making that classification the frame of her whole life (MEN).

BUILDING THE EMPIRE · VIII · THE DESIGNATIONS, CONTINUED

What each woman is, continued

The Widowed Covenant
Because the designation belongs to the marriage, an Anointed widow holds a temporary status for two years that keeps most protections. She's expected to remarry within the class or drop to Stewardwife. The senior Anointed Wives run a network so efficient that no widow has ever fallen below Stewardwife (CR).
The six
INFERENCE Older notes describe a six-tier caste system. Leaving out the Ungoverned, the six are Anointed, Stewardwife, Bonded, Commonwife, Vowed and Unbound. OPEN QUESTION Is that the six, what's the official order, and where does Vowed rank?

BUILDING THE EMPIRE · VIII · BEING SORTED

Designation

Every girl is assessed and given an initial classification. In Aurem it happens at eighteen, by a Vowed officer, testing literacy, theological knowledge and "domestic aptitude." The result is the Unbound track or the elevated track, which means better education and a chance at Covenant Daughter consideration (DOM).

BUILDING THE EMPIRE · VIII · THE BINDING

Marriage as designation

The Binding is marriage as a legal and theological designation (TL). In Aurem it's held in a Temple, witnessed by a Vowed officiant, and registered with the Crown and the Temple at once. The bride wears white and gold and takes a surname hyphenated with her husband's. Ministers sign Binding certificates beneath each husband's name (DR3).

The Aurem Binding
Nobody asks if she consents. They ask if she is worthy. The correct answer is yes.
✥
DOM
Across the Dominions
Meridian's is the most legally elaborate, with registration that can take three weeks. Red Earth's is the most surveilled, with Vowed witnesses at every one. Mountain adds Crown officers. Sable Reach binds at dawn. Gulf keeps its music. In the Crownward a Commonwife Binding is a short registration at the Crown office, and an Administrative Class Binding is recorded for the Broadcast (DOM, CR).
The Imperial Binding
Seven public stages: the Declaration, Covenant Week, the Anointing (a private ceremony on the sixth day in which the Prelate anoints the bride), the Binding Night, the Grand Binding (four hours in the Sanctum), the Procession down Sovereign Boulevard, and the Private Supper, five guests at most. The bride's vows are twice as long as the heir's. The Empire calls that theology. In practice it's a legal document spoken aloud in a sacred building (CR).
Names
OPEN QUESTION Does the hyphenated surname apply only in Aurem? Celeste never took a Binding name, so she's still a Parish (AM). Miriam's would be something like Parish-Osei.

511
BUILDING THE EMPIRE · VIII · COMMONWIVES, ANOINTED WIVES, THE ARRANGEMENT

Two ends of the ladder

Commonwife marriage
How the Empire marries off the labor class. Aurem has the highest Commonwife rate, because the Empire made marriage look like the highest honor available there (DOM). A Commonwife is expected to conceive within the Covenant timeline. A Vowed officer conducts quarterly household assessments, and two years without a pregnancy brings informal comments first and then Temple review (MEN, TL). (Volume Two, Leah.)
Reassignment
When a husband abandons his Commonwife, she can be reassigned to another man's household. It happened to Miriam after Thomas left (FR 0). Thomas left voluntarily (FR 3). Celeste has no proof Miriam is alive (FR 16). OPEN QUESTION Who decides it, what is her status during it, and why did Solomon keep the Osei name?
Anointed Wives
All thirty-nine Trials place Anointed wives, and the Crown contest is a layer on top. One woman goes to the heir, the other eleven are married down the elite ladder, and nobody goes home unassigned (FR 19). The husbands are chosen by the oversight committee and ranked by standing. A man can't reject the woman assigned to him, but he can decline the assignment altogether, which removes him from the ladder. It has happened twice (FR 28).
The Arrangement
Among the Anointed Court, male headship is mostly performance. Wives manage the information, relationships and social intelligence that make their husbands' positions work, and surface the right idea in private so the husband can present it in public. They never take credit or contradict him in public. New Anointed Wives learn it in the first year. Lyris runs the whole network, inherited from the Anointed Wife who mentored her. Celeste told Eden about the Arrangement before she left Aurem (CR).

INFERENCE Ladies in Waiting, the third- and fourth-place finishers, stay in the Crownward in supporting roles (TR). Under FR 19 they'd also be married into elite households. Both hold if they're placed in Crownward households and serve the Anointed Wife too.

TKC: DOM · TL · MEN · FR 0 · FR 3 · FR 16 · FR 19 · FR 28 · CR · TR

BUILDING THE EMPIRE · VIII · THE HOUSEHOLD ON PAPER

Unbound, Ungoverned, and four households

Unbound
"Difficult and surveilled" (TL). An Unbound woman can head a household only through reclassification, and household paperwork still demands an adult male authority.
Children
The Empire classifies children by household registration, not parentage or appearance (FR 32). Hannah's daughter Naomi is eligible through Hannah's household and standing, whatever her father's status (FR 31).
The Ungoverned
Women who chose exile over designation. Their deepest roots are in the Ardenne hollows, where some communities have lived outside the system for two or three generations. The Empire calls them criminals, and the mountain people call them something closer to saints. Crossings happen through the Sable desert. Lone interprets "Ungoverned" loosely and never files a full report. Saint Meridian has the largest population of sympathizers, people inside the system who leave gaps in it on purpose (DOM).

519
THE TWELVE DOMINIONS · 1 · AUREM

Aurem

Aurelia, formerly Atlanta · Georgia, Alabama, Mississippi, Tennessee, South Carolina, North Carolina

SEAL OF AUREM · DRAW OR PASTE
In the Trials
15 Anointed Wives in 38 Trials, the most of any Dominion
Designation
At 18, by a Vowed officer
39th Covenant Daughter
Eden Selah Osei · Aurelia · 21

Atlanta had Black wealth, Black culture, Black education and Black memory built so deep into the city that the Coalition couldn't flatten it without admitting what it was erasing. So the Empire dressed it up and called it restoration. The BeltLine became a ceremonial promenade lined with statues and Broadcast screens. Historic neighborhoods became Stewardwife districts, and Peachtree Street became Covenant Way. Magnolias were planted everywhere because they photograph well and rot slowly. Aurelia is the Empire's favorite mirror, Atlanta wearing somebody else's clothes, and the most surveilled and showcased territory in the Empire (TL).

It's humid from May through October. Storms leave the streets smelling of pine and wet concrete, and the red clay keeps bleeding through the paving. Black culture went deepest underground here and survived most stubbornly. Imperial hymns play on Covenant Way, and something else plays in the outer districts. Food is the main vessel of memory, and the Empire never thought the kitchen was dangerous enough to watch.

Aurem has the highest Commonwife rate and the most elaborate marriage ceremonies in the Empire, on purpose. Its Covenant Daughter gets forty-seven seconds on Sovereign Boulevard in the Opening Procession, more than anyone else, and every girl in Aurem knows that number before she can read. An Aurem candidate is expected to win. Death is the place where "the Empire lost completely and still has no idea."

Rites the Empire doesn't run

The Salt Ceremony · the old way of burial (the Telling, the Feeding, the Release) · the Speaking · kitchens

TKC: DOM · TR · TL
SEAT: AURELIA
FORMERLY ATLANTA
520

THE TWELVE DOMINIONS · 2 · GULF

Gulf

Port Seraphine, formerly New Orleans · Louisiana, the Mississippi Gulf Coast, Florida

SEAL OF GULF · DRAW OR PASTE
In the Trials
Historically penalized for warmth
Designation
At 14, the Procession
39th Covenant Daughter
Delphine Fontenot · Port Seraphine · 22

New Orleans was never going to be easy. The first Governor-Prelate lasted fourteen months and reported the city "resistant to spiritual reorientation." The Empire settled for managing it. Port Seraphine is loyal enough for the Broadcast and layered enough underground that the Vowed have learned to look away. Hurricane season takes half the year, and the city is "built on water and spite."

Gulf is the most culturally intact Dominion. Music survived here, standing in plain sight, because tourism, rebranded as "cultural pilgrimage," earns too much to lose. Gulf candidates arrive fluent in being watched, and the scoring system has historically penalized them for warmth, which Crown advisors read as performance. A Gulf woman who knows the gap between what she shows and what she keeps is one of the most dangerous women in the room.

THE TWELVE DOMINIONS · 3 · ARDENNE

Ardenne

Ashford, formerly Charleston, West Virginia · West Virginia, Virginia, Kentucky, Appalachia

SEAL OF ARDENNE · DRAW OR PASTE
In the Trials
Rarely produces Covenant Daughters
Designation
Standard in Ashford. Hollow children tested when they can be brought in.
39th Covenant Daughter
Maren Cole · Ashford · 23

Appalachia was never fully inside America's idea of itself, and it isn't fully inside the Empire's. Ashford is the city the Empire keeps as proof that Ardenne is pacified, and everything past the tree line says otherwise. It has real winters, hollows that cut communities off from each other and from surveillance, and fog thick enough to hide the next house.

The Ungoverned have their deepest roots here. Outer Ardenne children who get assessed score extraordinarily well, since children raised to solve real problems do well on problem-solving tests. Ardenne rarely sends a Covenant Daughter, and when it does she's usually from Ashford and quiet about the gap. Its Governor-Prelate has mostly stopped trying to fix the marriage records from outside Ashford.

THE TWELVE DOMINIONS · 4 · MERIDIAN

Meridian

Blessed Shore, formerly Philadelphia · Pennsylvania, Maryland, New Jersey, Delaware

SEAL OF MERIDIAN · DRAW OR PASTE
In the Trials
Extremely prepared candidates
Designation
Three-day sessions: theology, written reasoning, policy, "relational compliance"
39th Covenant Daughter
Corinne Vale · Blessed Shore · 22

The Empire took Philadelphia for its symbolism. The city that held the Declaration of Independence now holds the legal architecture of everything the Declaration claimed to prevent. Blessed Shore houses the Covenant Court's secondary chambers, where the law "gets interpreted, refined, and weaponized."

Meridian absorbed Imperial culture more completely than almost anywhere, because its administrators trust process. Its underground is made of lawyers and bureaucrats filing precise challenges that go nowhere and documenting them for a future someone is preparing. Stewardwives here are expert readers of Binding contracts, trained in Reading Circles. Meridian candidates know every rule and where the gaps are, which makes one who decides to move almost impossible to stop.

THE TWELVE DOMINIONS · 5 · VANE

Vane

Saint Meridian, formerly New York City · New York and New England

SEAL OF VANE · DRAW OR PASTE
In the Trials
The legacy Dominion, with the most Anointed Wives after Aurem
Designation
At 16, the Broadcast Assessment, partly filmed
39th Covenant Daughter
Calla Merritt · Saint Meridian · 21

New York was the hardest cultural conquest and the one the Empire brags about most. It named the whole Dominion after its founding Emperor, which is both honor and leash. Saint Meridian is the Broadcast capital, and the studios that announce Trial scores sit in old Midtown. The skyline stayed, and everything underneath it changed.

The city's density makes surveillance both total and porous, and it has the largest Ungoverned-sympathizer population of any seat city. Vane is the legacy Dominion. Some families have sent Covenant Daughters to three consecutive Trials, and high-scoring girls are prepared for candidacy from the time they can read.

THE TWELVE DOMINIONS · 6 · IRONGATE

Irongate

Iron Benediction, formerly Chicago · Illinois, Indiana, Ohio, Michigan, Wisconsin

SEAL OF IRONGATE · DRAW OR PASTE
In the Trials
Almost always administrative daughters
Designation
At 14 the Placement, at 18 Imperial Designation
39th Covenant Daughter
Nadia Orin · Iron Benediction · 20

Chicago gave the Empire industry and political machinery, which it kept, and music, which it tried to dismantle. Blues and jazz were labeled "morally ambiguous musical forms" in 2053 and went underground. Iron Benediction is the manufacturing heart of the Empire.

Irongate is the most class-divided Dominion, and its labor and administrative districts might as well be different civilizations. The labor-district women run a mutual aid network called the Kitchen that the Vowed have never mapped. Covenant Daughters are almost always administrative daughters, because selection rewards polish and polish costs money. Nadia Orin is the exception, the most working-class Covenant Daughter in Trial history (TR).

THE TWELVE DOMINIONS · 7 · HEARTLAND

Heartland

Providence Cross, formerly Kansas City · Missouri, Kansas, Iowa, Nebraska, Minnesota, the Dakotas

SEAL OF HEARTLAND · DRAW OR PASTE
In the Trials
The most theologically fluent
Designation
At 13, the First Covenant, where her track is set in public
39th Covenant Daughter
Ruth Gideon · Providence Cross · 22

The Heartland didn't need conquering, because the Coalition had been building here for decades. Providence Cross is the most genuinely loyal seat city in the Empire. People sing the hymns because they mean them.

The sincerity is the Empire's most valuable asset and one of its hardest to control, because real believers have opinions about what faithfulness requires. Some of the Empire's most serious theological debates happen here, including Heartland women's "theological inquiry" into the Commonwife designation. Heartland sends the most theologically fluent Covenant Daughters, for whom devotion is both credential and liability.

THE TWELVE DOMINIONS · 8 · RED EARTH

Red Earth

Canaan's Gate, formerly Oklahoma City · Oklahoma, Arkansas, eastern Kansas, southwestern Missouri

SEAL OF RED EARTH · DRAW OR PASTE
In the Trials
Often scored mid-pack until it's too late
Designation
At 18, two weeks in Temple spaces, Crown facilities and "domestic simulation environments"
39th Covenant Daughter
Delia Cross · Canaan's Gate · 24

Oklahoma's history of land taken and people removed came before the Empire. Canaan's Gate is built over a city that survived the 1995 bombing, and the Empire likes that resilience story. Red Earth piloted the Commonwife quota in 2058, and its women have lived under the full version longest.

It's the most visibly religious Dominion in its architecture and the most privately exhausted, with scripture on every building and the Vowed embedded as "pastoral care." Under the performance runs a language of small signals refined over decades. Red Earth candidates are the best in the Empire at performing compliance while thinking something else. Delia Cross's family was transferred there from Aurem when she was six, and she carries a grudge against the Dominion she left (TR).

THE TWELVE DOMINIONS · 9 · LONE

Lone

Covenant Basin, formerly Houston · Texas, New Mexico, western Louisiana

SEAL OF LONE · DRAW OR PASTE
In the Trials
Pragmatic about the outcome
Designation
At 16 in Covenant Basin. The First Ride outside it.
39th Covenant Daughter
Vivienne Hale · Covenant Basin · 23

Texas negotiated its surrender. The Empire got the territory and Texas kept the attitude. Covenant Basin runs on oil wealth and space infrastructure. Its Governor-Prelate is always Texas-born and has more independence than any other.

The distances leave real gaps in the surveillance grid, so west Lone is managed through local Vowed officers. People here perform loyalty loudly and then do what they were going to do anyway, and outer-territory women have a practical autonomy that would get them flagged in Aurem. Lone interprets "Ungoverned" loosely and never files a full report. It treats the Trials like any other Imperial requirement.

THE TWELVE DOMINIONS · 10 · MOUNTAIN

Mountain

Crestfall, formerly Denver · Colorado, Wyoming, Montana, Idaho, parts of Utah and Nevada

SEAL OF MOUNTAIN · DRAW OR PASTE
In the Trials
Has never produced an Anointed Wife
Designation
At 16, with a physical assessment
39th Covenant Daughter
Petra Solano · Crestfall · 22

The Empire wanted the mountains for what's inside them: minerals, military installations, elevation and secrecy. Crestfall is the face, and the mountains are the truth. The classified operations there are the Empire's least documented and most important work: surveillance technology, resource extraction, Temple expansion funding and military capacity.

Crestfall women are the most physically capable in the Empire and among the most watched. Girls selected at Designation for classified Vowed service disappear from public life. Endurance was held in Mountain terrain for Trials 1 through 31, moved to a controlled facility for 32 through 38, and returned for the 39th without explanation. Azrael is hidden here, in a community whose patriarch owes the Vanes a debt (TL). Lyris is from Mountain too (WR).

THE TWELVE DOMINIONS · 11 · SABLE

Sable

Sable Reach, formerly Phoenix · Arizona, Nevada, New Mexico, parts of Utah

SEAL OF SABLE · DRAW OR PASTE
In the Trials
"Two Sable women are worth five from anywhere else"
Designation
At 18 in Sable Reach. The Finding outside it.
39th Covenant Daughter
Zara Voss · Sable Reach · 21 · desert name Tazaleh

The desert resists the Empire the way deserts resist everything, by not caring. Heat destroys equipment, sand gets into everything, and the distances defeat centralized monitoring. Sable Reach is the ornate Imperial city the Empire keeps as proof it can reach hard country. The desert beyond it is where Ungoverned crossings happen.

The desert held civilizations long before the United States, and the Empire renamed the cities but not the land. Women in the outer communities carry plant and land knowledge the Empire has never catalogued. Every Sable woman has two names, one for the Empire and one for the house. Zara Voss's desert name is Tazaleh (TL, TR). INTERPRETATION Sable's two-name custom and Celeste's house name for Eden are the same instinct in two Dominions, and Eden and Zara's name exchange in the middle of the book rests on it.

THE TWELVE DOMINIONS · 12 · PACIFIC

Pacific

Westhold, formerly Los Angeles · California, Oregon, parts of Nevada

SEAL OF PACIFIC · DRAW OR PASTE
In the Trials
Watched before they arrive
Designation
At 18. Every score reviewed in the Crownward for inherited resistance.
39th Covenant Daughter
Sera Montoya · Westhold · 24

Pacific carries the memory of resistance like scar tissue. The secession attempt and the two-year standoff produced a generation that remembers trying to leave, and their grandchildren know the story. The Empire watches Westhold harder than anywhere: more cameras per block, more loyalty programming, and a Governor-Prelate Dorian appointed personally, who performs warmth while administering iron.

Old Los Angeles's creative industries adapted instead of dying, and people still make what they actually want and pass it through networks the Empire can't map. Every Pacific girl's Designation is reviewed in the Crownward for inherited resistance, and every Pacific Covenant Daughter arrives already flagged.

THE COVENANT TRIALS · HOW TO USE THIS PART

Revelation, not competition

The Empire presents the Trials as revelation, never as competition, because "a competition can be rigged. A revelation cannot" (TR). The Broadcast's words are anointing, covenant, calling, worthy and chosen. This part covers how the Trials work in any year. What happens in the 39th belongs to Volume Two.

The Trials sit where every earlier part of this companion meets. The Temple runs the first phase and names it Genesis. The Crown runs the second and names it Exodus. The terrain runs the third. A girl is examined on scripture by people who've decided which scripture counts, debated on governance by the heir to a throne her own scripture limits, and sent into the mountains with no cameras by a Broadcast that will reconstruct what happened afterward.

"A competition can be rigged. A revelation cannot."
TRFR 19–28
The weights
Temple 30 · Crown 30 · Broadcast 40. Never disclosed.
The output
One Anointed Wife for the heir, eleven married down the ladder, nobody sent home unassigned (FR 19).
MARGINALIA
✥ See also: Part VI, the Triune Rule and the Binding.

THE COVENANT TRIALS · II · ELIGIBILITY AND NOMINATION

The Covenant Registry

Every Dominion keeps a Covenant Registry of women aged 18 to 25 who meet five requirements (TR): Unbound or elevated designation, no prior marriage registration, no Ungoverned citations, completion of the standard Imperial curriculum, and "demonstrated covenantal aptitude," which means whatever the Governor-Prelate needs it to mean.

INDEPENDENT
Self-nomination
The Empire frames it as exceptional faith. In practice the process is built to exhaust anyone without institutional support, and most self-nominations fail for that reason alone (TR). In Aurem it's "independent submission," a ten-dollar nonrefundable fee and a card printed in red instead of bordered in gold (DR3). Eden is the first Aurem candidate in the Trials' history to enter that way (TL).
Eligibility isn't entry
A House Mother can submit a name, and she isn't the only one who can (FR 27).

THE COVENANT TRIALS · III · THE REGIONAL ASSESSMENT

Three days, a ranked list

Each Dominion runs a three-day Regional Assessment under senior Vowed officers, which produces a ranked list (TR, TL).

Ministerial review
Some sections, like Discernment and Spiritual Fitness, are graded by ministerial review, so a girl can know every answer and still fail for a lack of humility (DR3).
The choice
The Governor-Prelate chooses the Covenant Daughter from the top three. In practice he picks whoever the Crown has quietly signaled it prefers, then documents it as a choice from the top three regardless of the real ranking (TR).

THE COVENANT TRIALS · IV · ARRIVAL AND THE COVENANT HOUSES

Covenant Row

Arrival
The twelve arrive in the first week of summer, staggered across seven days so each arrival can be released as its own day of content. Each travels by Dominion transport with two Appointed Officers and her House Mother (TR). Each walks Sovereign Boulevard on camera. The walk is choreographed: what pace to keep, where to look, how to hold her hands (CR).
The Houses
Twelve townhouses on Covenant Row, one per Dominion, under five minutes' walk from Sovereign Boulevard. Cameras in every common area, entrance and garden, and not the bedrooms (CR). The candidates aren't sequestered. They're embedded in Crownward public life: Temple services, forums, the approved dining rooms, the Administrative Side markets (CR).
House staff
In House Aurem, the House Mother, the House Coordinator, the stylist and the Debate and Protocol Advisor (TL). A House hosts one Covenant Daughter per Trial, and staff serve across many. Hannah's "six Trials, twelve girls" counts her whole career across several Houses and posts (FR 26).
HOUSE AUREM · COVENANT ROW · FLOOR PLAN, DRAW IT
TKC: TR · CR · TL · FR 26

THE COVENANT TRIALS · V · THE THREE PHASES

Three powers, three questions

The Trials run in three phases. Each belongs to a different power and asks a different question, and they line up with the novel's Genesis, Exodus and Revelation (TR).

I · GENESIS
The Covenant Examination
"Who are you?"
The Temple · weeks 1–4
  • The Settling
  • The Doctrinal Assessment
  • The Theological Interview
  • The Public Testimony

The Governance Trials
"How smart are you?"
The Crown · weeks 5–10
  • The Dominion Forum
  • The Civic Debates
  • The Anointed Evening
  • The Pairing Introduction
30%

Endurance
"What are you willing to endure?"
The terrain · three days
  • Coordinates to reach
  • No schedule
  • No handlers

feeds the Broadcast's 40%

SUPERSEDED The original Trials file had four phases: the Covenant Examination, the Governance Trials, the Public Season and the Declaration. OPEN QUESTION The original also had candidates sign a confidentiality covenant before the Crown's phase, with removal and a permanent Ungoverned citation for breaking it (TR-orig). Does it still exist?

Which phase each of the twelve is built to survive
TKC: TR
552
THE COVENANT TRIALS · V · THE PHASES IN DETAIL

What happens in each

The Settling
Presented as a grace period. It's the most intensive assessment of all, because the Vowed watch everything.
The Doctrinal Assessment
A six-hour written exam in the Temple Sanctum, set at each candidate's ceiling, so no two girls get the same questions.
The Theological Interview
A conversation with a senior Vowed officer, who's told to pursue any rehearsed answer until she finds what's underneath it.
The Public Testimony
A fifteen-minute statement of faith and purpose, broadcast live, the first time the Empire hears each candidate unmediated. Candidates are given Vowed handlers to "ensure theological accuracy," and they can decline them.
The Dominion Forum
Every remaining candidate in a room with an Empire-wide crisis and no assigned leader.
The Civic Debates
Three filmed debates on governance, theology and social policy, topics given an hour before. The Crown Heir moderates the social policy debate.
The Anointed Evening
Four hours with the Anointed Court in the Crown Palace, where the cameras can't follow into the side rooms.
The Pairing Introduction
Thirty minutes alone with the Heir, with a Crown advisor and a Vowed officer present and a camera outside the door. The room isn't recorded. The Commission has been known to wire candidates beforehand.
Endurance
What the Empire calls covenantal fortitude and the terrain calls survival. The Broadcast films the deployment and the final checkpoint, and everything between is reconstructed from the candidates' accounts, always incomplete.
A Doctrinal Assessment question at Eden's ceiling. Write one
TKC: TR
