THE COVENANT TRIALS · VI · SCORING

The Evening Reckoning

Sovereign Boulevard · every evening at seven
The scores update on the screens.
✥
THE EMPIRE NAMED IT THE EVENING RECKONING (TR)
The composite
Temple 30 percent, Crown 30 percent, Broadcast 40 percent, never disclosed. The Broadcast's share includes narrative-priority decisions by the Broadcast Director (TR).
The antagonist narrative
Every Trial produces one, assigned to the candidate who most threatens a comfortable outcome, and not to the eventual winner (TR).
The Eve problem
When a candidate's intelligence becomes the story instead of her compliance, the Broadcast calls it internally the Eve problem. The narrative implies and never accuses. It moves the score without changing a single formal assessment, and it has worked every time it's been used (TR).
What the narrative-priority decision looks like inside the Citadel, and who signs it
TKC: TR
555
THE COVENANT TRIALS · VII · THE DECLARATION AND ITS RULES

Sovereign Boulevard, live

After the Endurance rankings are confirmed, the Crown Heir names his Anointed Wife in a public ceremony on Sovereign Boulevard, broadcast live in all twelve Dominions (TR). The rules around that moment come from the Final Rulings.

The ranked winner · FR 20
The heir may choose any of the twelve. "Without deviation" describes the record, not a rule.
What winning means · FR 21
Being the choice that costs the Crown nothing. The rankings tell the heir which selection is free. Deviation is permitted and expensive, and no heir has ever paid it.
Refusal · FR 22
The recommendation stands by default once the window closes. Refusing is abstention, not resistance, and produces the same result as complying.
Cancellation · FR 23
Impossible once the Trial is announced, except in a public dynastic emergency: the Heir's death or incapacity, or the Emperor's death forcing an immediate transition. A candidate's death doesn't downgrade a Trial.
A candidate's death · FR 24
Her status goes back to her family, with no compensation and no punishment, as though the designation had never been made.
Rejected candidates · FR 25
Married down as designated. The rejection follows them "not in the record, in the room."
Ladies in Waiting · TR
The third- and fourth-place Endurance finishers stay in the Crownward in supporting roles to the Anointed Wife and the Crown household.
What the men assigned to the eleven know about their brides before the Declaration
TKC: TR · FR 20–25

BACK MATTER · SUPERSEDED AND IN CONFLICT

What no longer controls, and where the files disagree

SUPERSEDED · the old companion's Prelate
Page 182 of the old companion marked "Prelate Huldah's office" as canon. FR 33 renamed the Prelate. Huldah is only the biblical prophet.
SUPERSEDED · Solomon at thirteen
The timeline's age. DECIDED sixteen.
SUPERSEDED · Dorian at 61
The Crownward file's age. The ruling uses 59.
SUPERSEDED · Crest as Dorian's father
The Crownward file. Under FR 35, his uncle.
SUPERSEDED · four phases
The original Trials file's Public Season and Declaration phases. The updated file has three.
CONFLICT · the Commonwife quotas
Introduced under Crest in 2062 (TL), piloted in Red Earth in 2058 (DOM). INFERENCE A pilot under Elias, made Empire-wide under Crest.
CONFLICT · Sera Callum's origin
"The Northern Dominion" (TL). No Dominion has that name.
CORRECTION TO THE OLD COMPANION Corrections to the old companion's research run through every part, each printed where it falls under the heading "Correction to the old companion."
A conflict I've found since this printing

BACK MATTER · SOURCES AND EDITIONS · 1 OF 5

Where all of this came from

Every page carries a narrow source margin on its outer edge, in compressed form, author and short title. The compressed forms point to the full citations on the next pages. Nothing in a margin is a citation by itself.

Research
Unlabeled prose in Parts I to IV is research: the text says it, or the manuscript evidence shows it, and you can check it in any study Bible with a critical apparatus.
CONTESTED
Responsible scholars or traditions disagree. The dispute is printed, with the strongest case on each side, and no verdict.
INTERPRETATION · INFERENCE
A reading offered as a reading, or a conclusion drawn from your files that they don't state.
POSSIBLE
A TKC construction, about Dominion, the Temple or the manuscript, and not a claim about scripture. Test it before use.
DECIDED
A call you've made. Fixed until you rule otherwise.
On the secondary literature

The works listed are standard reference points and not endorsements of any one position. Where two disagree, and several do sharply, the companion prints the disagreement. Several are technical commentaries that assume Hebrew or Greek. You don't need them to use this book. They're listed so every claim can be traced.

Translation I'm working fromEdition or printingUsed for

BACK MATTER · SOURCES AND EDITIONS · 2 OF 5 · HEBREW BIBLE

Texts, lexicons and commentaries

MT
Biblia Hebraica Stuttgartensia. Stuttgart: Deutsche Bibelgesellschaft. The standard printed Masoretic text. Cite the printing you use.
LXX
Rahlfs–Hanhart, Septuaginta. Deutsche Bibelgesellschaft. Consulted where the Greek differs from the Hebrew.
DSS
Dead Sea Scroll manuscripts cited by siglum, such as 4QDeut and 4Q365.
BDB
Brown, Driver and Briggs, A Hebrew and English Lexicon of the Old Testament. Oxford: Clarendon, 1906.
HALOT
Koehler and Baumgartner, The Hebrew and Aramaic Lexicon of the Old Testament. Leiden: Brill. The current standard. Use it over BDB where they conflict.
Milgrom
Jacob Milgrom, Leviticus. Anchor Bible 3, 3A, 3B. New York: Doubleday, 1991–2001.
Klawans
Jonathan Klawans, Impurity and Sin in Ancient Judaism. Oxford University Press, 2000.
Douglas
Mary Douglas, Purity and Danger. Routledge, 1966, and Leviticus as Literature. Oxford, 1999.
Olyan
Saul M. Olyan, Disability in the Hebrew Bible. Cambridge University Press, 2008.
Bergsma
John Sietze Bergsma, The Jubilee from Leviticus to Qumran. Leiden: Brill, 2007.
Weinfeld
Moshe Weinfeld, Deuteronomy 1–11. Anchor Bible 5. Doubleday, 1991, and Deuteronomy and the Deuteronomic School. Oxford: Clarendon, 1972.
Levinson
Bernard M. Levinson, Deuteronomy and the Hermeneutics of Legal Innovation. Oxford University Press, 1997.
Goldenberg
David M. Goldenberg, The Curse of Ham. Princeton University Press, 2003.

Greek texts, Jesus research, ancient neighbors

NA28
Novum Testamentum Graece, 28th revised edition. Deutsche Bibelgesellschaft, 2012. Where the companion says a reading is absent from the oldest manuscripts, this is the apparatus it means.
UBS5
The Greek New Testament, 5th revised edition. United Bible Societies, 2014.
Sanders
E. P. Sanders, The Historical Figure of Jesus. Penguin, 1993.
Ehrman
Bart D. Ehrman, The New Testament: A Historical Introduction. Oxford University Press. Record your edition, since page references move.

Mark Goodacre, The Case Against Q. Trinity Press International, 2002.
Borg and Crossan
Marcus Borg and John Dominic Crossan, The Last Week. HarperOne, 2006.
Levine
Amy-Jill Levine, The Misunderstood Jew. HarperOne, 2006.
Epp
Eldon Jay Epp, Junia: The First Woman Apostle. Fortress, 2005.
COS
William W. Hallo and K. Lawson Younger, eds., The Context of Scripture. Brill, 1997–2002. The flood parallels, the Mesha inscription and the law codes.
SAA 2
Simo Parpola and Kazuko Watanabe, Neo-Assyrian Treaties and Loyalty Oaths. Helsinki University Press, 1988. The succession-treaty curses compared with Deuteronomy 28.
Steymans
Hans Ulrich Steymans, Deuteronomium 28 und die adê zur Thronfolgeregelung Asarhaddons. 1995.
Mendenhall · Moran
George Mendenhall, "Covenant Forms in Israelite Tradition" (1954–55). William Moran, "The Ancient Near Eastern Background of the Love of God in Deuteronomy" (1963).
